     1→# Memory
     2→
     3→## User Preferences
     4→
     5→- **Commit approval**: Always present the full commit commands to the user for physical review BEFORE executing them. Do not run jj commit / git commit until the user explicitly approves.
     6→- **Versioning**: Every plan must start with a version bump per notes/README.md "Versioning during development". Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step approach before starting.
     7→- **Pre-commit checklist**: Before committing, run: tests, clippy, fmt, install the app, then retest.
     8→
     9→## Feedback
    10→
    11→- [Always include ochid trailer in commits](feedback_ochid_trailer.md)
    12→- [Always commit .claude repo before finalize](feedback_claude_commit_before_finalize.md)
    13→- [Skip .claude push before finalize — finalize handles it](feedback_no_claude_push.md)
    14→- [Finalize must be absolute last action — only status line after](feedback_finalize_last.md)
    15→- [Use --ignore-immutable for late squash after push](feedback_squash_ignore_immutable.md)
    16→- [Use vc-x1 chid to retrieve changeIDs, not jj log](feedback_use_chid.md)
    17→- [Recognize when stuck — 5+ min thinking on simple task means stuck](feedback_stuck_detection.md)
    18→- [No --allow-new flag for jj git push](feedback_jj_no_allow_new.md)
    19→- [No text output after finalize — trailing writes miss squash window](feedback_no_finalize_echo.md)
    20→- [Ask for clarification on ambiguous input instead of spinning](feedback_ask_on_ambiguity.md)
    21→- ["finalize steps" triggers full session-end workflow](feedback_finalize_steps.md)
    22→
    23→## Lessons Learned
    24→
    25→- **Avoid duplicate commits in .claude repo**: Committing the bot session repo multiple times in one session can create duplicate-titled commits with other session commits interleaved, making squashing non-trivial. Prefer a single commit to .claude at session end.
    26→- **ochid trailers need updating after squash**: When squashing changes the target commit in .claude, the ochid reference in the app repo must be updated to point at the correct changeID.
    27→- **Don't modify .claude repo programmatically mid-session**: Session data updates continuously, so bot-initiated changes to .claude can create circular modifications.
    28→
    29→## Key Files
    30→
    31→- notes/README.md — contains versioning workflow, todo format rules
    32→- notes/todo.md — near-term task tracking
    33→- notes/chores-01.md — chore details and reference links
    34→- CLAUDE.md — dual repo commit/push workflow
    35→

Here's a summary of what I know from our previous conversations:

## Project: vc-x1
A Rust CLI tool for working with jj (Jujutsu) version control. The project has evolved rapidly from v0.1 through **v0.18.1** (current).

## Key Feature History
- **v0.6** — `finalize` subcommand (daemonize, squash/push logic, `--ignore-immutable`)
- **v0.7–0.9** — Refactoring (extract modules, clap derive migration), `desc` subcommand
- **v0.10** — `--revision`, `--repo`, `--limit` options for `desc` and `list`
- **v0.11–0.12** — `chid` subcommand (show changeID/commitID)
- **v0.13** — `--limit` for `chid`
- **v0.14** — Positional `..` notation, `finalize --bookmark`
- **v0.15** — Bold primary revision output
- **v0.16** — `--indent`/`-i` for desc body lines
- **v0.17** — Replace `--foreground` with `--detach` for finalize
- **v0.18** — `show` subcommand with header, bookmarks, diff summary, `..` notation, file limiting

## Workflow Feedback I've Accumulated
- **Always get approval** before running commit commands
- **Include ochid trailer** in commits
- **Use `vc-x1 chid`** to retrieve changeIDs (not `jj log`)
- **Finalize must be the absolute last action** — no text output after
- **Commit .claude repo before finalize** (finalize handles the push)
- **Use `--ignore-immutable`** for late squash after push
- **Ask for clarification** on ambiguous input rather than spinning
- **Recognize when stuck** — 5+ min on a simple task means step back

## Dual-Repo Workflow
The project uses two jj repos (app at `.` and bot session at `.claude`) with a specific commit/push/finalize workflow documented in CLAUDE.md.

Is there anything specific from past sessions you'd like to revisit or discuss?

     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→use std::sync::Arc;
     3→
     4→use chrono::Offset;
     5→use clap::Args;
     6→use jj_lib::backend::TreeValue;
     7→use jj_lib::commit::Commit;
     8→use jj_lib::matchers::EverythingMatcher;
     9→use jj_lib::merged_tree::TreeDiffIterator;
    10→use jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;
    11→use jj_lib::repo::{ReadonlyRepo, Repo};
    12→use jj_lib::workspace::Workspace;
    13→use pollster::FutureExt;
    14→
    15→use crate::common;
    16→
    17→/// Parsed file limit: None (suppress), Some(n) (cap at n), or all.
    18→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    19→pub enum FileLimit {
    20→    /// Don't show changed files section at all.
    21→    None,
    22→    /// Show up to N files (with first/last split if truncated).
    23→    Cap(usize),
    24→    /// Show all files.
    25→    All,
    26→}
    27→
    28→impl FileLimit {
    29→    fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self, String> {
    30→        match s {
    31→            "0" => Ok(FileLimit::None),
    32→            "all" => Ok(FileLimit::All),
    33→            _ => s
    34→                .parse::<usize>()
    35→                .map(FileLimit::Cap)
    36→                .map_err(|_| format!("invalid file limit '{s}': expected number, 0, or 'all'")),
    37→        }
    38→    }
    39→}
    40→
    41→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
    42→pub struct ShowArgs {
    43→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    44→    #[arg()]
    45→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    46→
    47→    /// Count (commits per dotted side)
    48→    #[arg()]
    49→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    50→
    51→    /// Revision to show (default: @)
    52→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    53→    pub revision: String,
    54→
    55→    /// Max changed files: number, 0 (none), or 'all' (default: 50)
    56→    #[arg(short = 'f', long = "files", default_value = "50")]
    57→    pub files: String,
    58→
    59→    /// Maximum number of commits to show
    60→    #[arg(short, long)]
    61→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    62→
    63→    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)
    64→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    65→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    66→}
    67→
    68→pub fn show(args: &ShowArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    69→    let file_limit = FileLimit::parse(&args.files)?;
    70→
    71→    let resolved = common::resolve_dot_args(
    72→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    73→        args.pos_count,
    74→        &args.revision,
    75→        args.limit,
    76→        "@",
    77→    );
    78→
    79→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    80→    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.revset)?;
    81→
    82→    if commit_ids.is_empty() {
    83→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", resolved.revset).into());
    84→    }
    85→
    86→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
    87→
    88→    // Resolve the primary commit ID for highlighting
    89→    let primary_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.primary_rev)?;
    90→    let primary_id = primary_ids.first();
    91→
    92→    if let Some(both_count) = resolved.both_count {
    93→        show_both(
    94→            &workspace,
    95→            &repo,
    96→            args,
    97→            both_count,
    98→            file_limit,
    99→            &root_commit_id,
   100→        )?;
   101→        return Ok(());
   102→    }
   103→
   104→    let mut count = 0;
   105→    let mut first = true;
   106→    for commit_id in &commit_ids {
   107→        if *commit_id == root_commit_id {
   108→            continue;
   109→        }
   110→
   111→        if !first {
   112→            // Separator between commits
   113→            println!("────────────────────────────────────────");
   114→        }
   115→        first = false;
   116→
   117→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   118→        let is_primary = primary_id == Some(commit_id);
   119→
   120→        show_one_commit(&commit, &workspace, &repo, file_limit, is_primary)?;
   121→
   122→        count += 1;
   123→        if let Some(limit) = resolved.limit
   124→            && count >= limit
   125→        {
   126→            break;
   127→        }
   128→    }
   129→
   130→    Ok(())
   131→}
   132→
   133→fn show_both(
   134→    workspace: &Workspace,
   135→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   136→    args: &ShowArgs,
   137→    both_count: usize,
   138→    file_limit: FileLimit,
   139→    root_commit_id: &jj_lib::backend::CommitId,
   140→) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   141→    let bare_rev = args.pos_rev.as_deref().unwrap_or("@");
   142→    let spec = common::parse_dot_rev(bare_rev);
   143→    let anchor_ids = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &spec.rev)?;
   144→    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {
   145→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", spec.rev).into());
   146→    }
   147→    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];
   148→
   149→    let ancestor_ids = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("::{}", spec.rev))?;
   150→    let descendant_ids = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("{}::", spec.rev))?;
   151→
   152→    // Descendants (newest first), excluding anchor
   153→    let mut desc_ids: Vec<_> = descendant_ids
   154→        .iter()
   155→        .filter(|id| **id != *root_commit_id && **id != *anchor_id)
   156→        .collect();
   157→    if both_count > 0 {
   158→        let start = desc_ids.len().saturating_sub(both_count);
   159→        desc_ids = desc_ids[start..].to_vec();
   160→    } else {
   161→        desc_ids.clear();
   162→    }
   163→
   164→    // Ancestors (newest first), excluding anchor
   165→    let mut anc_ids: Vec<_> = Vec::new();
   166→    if both_count > 0 {
   167→        let mut c = 0;
   168→        for id in &ancestor_ids {
   169→            if *id == *root_commit_id || *id == *anchor_id {
   170→                continue;
   171→            }
   172→            anc_ids.push(id);
   173→            c += 1;
   174→            if c >= both_count {
   175→                break;
   176→            }
   177→        }
   178→    }
   179→
   180→    let mut first = true;
   181→    for id in &desc_ids {
   182→        if !first {
   183→            println!("────────────────────────────────────────");
   184→        }
   185→        first = false;
   186→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(id)?;
   187→        show_one_commit(&commit, workspace, repo, file_limit, false)?;
   188→    }
   189→
   190→    if !first {
   191→        println!("────────────────────────────────────────");
   192→    }
   193→    first = false;
   194→    let anchor_commit = repo.store().get_commit(anchor_id)?;
   195→    show_one_commit(&anchor_commit, workspace, repo, file_limit, true)?;
   196→
   197→    for id in &anc_ids {
   198→        if !first {
   199→            println!("────────────────────────────────────────");
   200→        }
   201→        first = false;
   202→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(id)?;
   203→        show_one_commit(&commit, workspace, repo, file_limit, false)?;
   204→    }
   205→
   206→    Ok(())
   207→}
   208→
   209→fn show_one_commit(
   210→    commit: &Commit,
   211→    workspace: &Workspace,
   212→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   213→    file_limit: FileLimit,
   214→    is_primary: bool,
   215→) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   216→    // Ids
   217→    let ids_line = common::format_commit_short(commit);
   218→    if is_primary {
   219→        println!("Ids:       {}", common::bold(&ids_line));
   220→    } else {
   221→        println!("Ids:       {ids_line}");
   222→    }
   223→
   224→    // Author
   225→    let author = commit.author();
   226→    println!(
   227→        "Author:    {} <{}> ({})",
   228→        author.name,
   229→        author.email,
   230→        format_timestamp(&author.timestamp)
   231→    );
   232→
   233→    // Committer
   234→    let committer = commit.committer();
   235→    println!(
   236→        "Committer: {} <{}> ({})",
   237→        committer.name,
   238→        committer.email,
   239→        format_timestamp(&committer.timestamp)
   240→    );
   241→
   242→    // Parents
   243→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
   244→    for parent_id in commit.parent_ids() {
   245→        if *parent_id == root_commit_id {
   246→            continue;
   247→        }
   248→        let parent = repo.store().get_commit(parent_id)?;
   249→        println!("Parent:    {}", common::format_commit_short(&parent));
   250→    }
   251→
   252→    // Children
   253→    let children_ids =
   254→        common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &format!("children({})", commit.id().hex()))?;
   255→    for child_id in &children_ids {
   256→        if *child_id == root_commit_id {
   257→            continue;
   258→        }
   259→        let child = repo.store().get_commit(child_id)?;
   260→        println!("Child:     {}", common::format_commit_short(&child));
   261→    }
   262→
   263→    // Branches
   264→    let branches_str = format_branches(commit, workspace, repo)?;
   265→    println!("Branches:  {branches_str}");
   266→
   267→    // Follows
   268→    let follows = find_nearest_tag(commit, workspace, repo, true)?;
   269→    println!("Follows:   {}", follows.as_deref().unwrap_or(""));
   270→
   271→    // Precedes
   272→    let precedes = find_nearest_tag(commit, workspace, repo, false)?;
   273→    println!("Precedes:  {}", precedes.as_deref().unwrap_or(""));
   274→
   275→    // Description (body only — title is on the Ids line)
   276→    print_description(commit);
   277→
   278→    // Changed files
   279→    if file_limit != FileLimit::None {
   280→        print_diff(commit, repo, file_limit)?;
   281→    }
   282→
   283→    Ok(())
   284→}
   285→
   286→fn find_nearest_tag(
   287→    commit: &Commit,
   288→    workspace: &Workspace,
   289→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   290→    ancestor: bool,
   291→) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   292→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   293→
   294→    let revset_str = if ancestor {
   295→        format!("heads(tags() & ::{})", commit_hex)
   296→    } else {
   297→        format!("roots(tags() & {}::)", commit_hex)
   298→    };
   299→
   300→    let tag_ids = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &revset_str)?;
   301→    if tag_ids.is_empty() {
   302→        return Ok(None);
   303→    }
   304→
   305→    let tag_id = &tag_ids[0];
   306→    let view = repo.view();
   307→    for (name, local_remote) in view.tags() {
   308→        if local_remote.local_target.added_ids().any(|id| id == tag_id) {
   309→            return Ok(Some(name.as_str().to_string()));
   310→        }
   311→    }
   312→
   313→    Ok(None)
   314→}
   315→
   316→fn format_branches(
   317→    commit: &Commit,
   318→    workspace: &Workspace,
   319→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   320→) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   321→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   322→    let view = repo.view();
   323→    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   324→
   325→    for (name, local_remote) in view.bookmarks() {
   326→        for bookmark_id in local_remote.local_target.added_ids() {
   327→            let revset_str = format!("{} & ::{}", commit_hex, bookmark_id.hex());
   328→            let results = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &revset_str)?;
   329→            if !results.is_empty() {
   330→                names.push(name.as_str().to_string());
   331→                break;
   332→            }
   333→        }
   334→
   335→        for (remote_name, remote_ref) in &local_remote.remote_refs {
   336→            if remote_name.as_str() == "git" {
   337→                continue;
   338→            }
   339→            for bookmark_id in remote_ref.target.added_ids() {
   340→                let revset_str = format!("{} & ::{}", commit_hex, bookmark_id.hex());
   341→                let results = common::resolve_revset(workspace, repo, &revset_str)?;
   342→                if !results.is_empty() {
   343→                    names.push(format!("{}@{}", name.as_str(), remote_name.as_str()));
   344→                    break;
   345→                }
   346→            }
   347→        }
   348→    }
   349→
   350→    Ok(names.join(", "))
   351→}
   352→
   353→fn format_timestamp(ts: &jj_lib::backend::Timestamp) -> String {
   354→    let millis = ts.timestamp.0;
   355→    let secs = millis / 1000;
   356→    let tz_minutes = ts.tz_offset;
   357→
   358→    let dt = chrono::DateTime::from_timestamp(secs, 0)
   359→        .unwrap_or_default()
   360→        .with_timezone(
   361→            &chrono::FixedOffset::east_opt(tz_minutes * 60).unwrap_or(chrono::Utc.fix()),
   362→        );
   363→
   364→    dt.format("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S").to_string()
   365→}
   366→
   367→fn print_description(commit: &Commit) {
   368→    let desc = commit.description();
   369→    let body: String = desc
   370→        .lines()
   371→        .skip(1)
   372→        .skip_while(|l| l.is_empty())
   373→        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
   374→        .join("\n");
   375→
   376→    println!("Description:");
   377→    for line in body.lines() {
   378→        println!("    {line}");
   379→    }
   380→}
   381→
   382→fn print_diff(
   383→    commit: &Commit,
   384→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   385→    file_limit: FileLimit,
   386→) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   387→    let parent_tree = commit.parent_tree(repo.as_ref()).block_on()?;
   388→    let commit_tree = commit.tree();
   389→
   390→    let diff_iter = TreeDiffIterator::new(&parent_tree, &commit_tree, &EverythingMatcher);
   391→
   392→    let mut lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   393→    for entry in diff_iter {
   394→        let diff = entry.values?;
   395→        let before = diff.before.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();
   396→        let after = diff.after.as_resolved().cloned().flatten();
   397→
   398→        if matches!(&before, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_))) || matches!(&after, Some(TreeValue::Tree(_)))
   399→        {
   400→            continue;
   401→        }
   402→
   403→        let path_str = entry.path.as_internal_file_string().to_string();
   404→
   405→        let line = match (before, after) {
   406→            (None, Some(value)) => {
   407→                format!("    Added {} {path_str}", file_type_str(&value))
   408→            }
   409→            (Some(_), None) => {
   410→                format!(
   411→                    "    Removed {} {path_str}",
   412→                    file_type_str_from_merged(&diff.before),
   413→                )
   414→            }
   415→            (Some(_), Some(value)) => {
   416→                format!("    Modified {} {path_str}", file_type_str(&value))
   417→            }
   418→            (None, None) => continue,
   419→        };
   420→        lines.push(line);
   421→    }
   422→
   423→    let total = lines.len();
   424→    let cap = match file_limit {
   425→        FileLimit::All | FileLimit::None => 0,
   426→        FileLimit::Cap(n) => n,
   427→    };
   428→    let show_all = cap == 0 || total <= cap;
   429→
   430→    if show_all {
   431→        println!("Changed files: {total}");
   432→        for line in &lines {
   433→            println!("{line}");
   434→        }
   435→    } else {
   436→        let half = cap / 2;
   437→        let tail_start = total.saturating_sub(half);
   438→        let skipped = total - half - half;
   439→        println!("Changed files: {total} (showing {cap})");
   440→        for line in &lines[..half] {
   441→            println!("{line}");
   442→        }
   443→        println!("    ... {skipped} more ...");
   444→        for line in &lines[tail_start..] {
   445→            println!("{line}");
   446→        }
   447→    }
   448→
   449→    Ok(())
   450→}
   451→
   452→fn file_type_str(value: &TreeValue) -> &'static str {
   453→    match value {
   454→        TreeValue::File {
   455→            executable: false, ..
   456→        } => "regular file",
   457→        TreeValue::File {
   458→            executable: true, ..
   459→        } => "executable file",
   460→        TreeValue::Symlink(_) => "symlink",
   461→        TreeValue::Tree(_) => "tree",
   462→        TreeValue::GitSubmodule(_) => "git submodule",
   463→    }
   464→}
   465→
   466→fn file_type_str_from_merged(merged: &jj_lib::merge::Merge<Option<TreeValue>>) -> &'static str {
   467→    if let Some(Some(value)) = merged.as_resolved() {
   468→        file_type_str(value)
   469→    } else {
   470→        "file"
   471→    }
   472→}
   473→
```

> AGENT

The three functions form a dispatch hierarchy:

- **`show`** (line 68) — the **entry point** called from main. It parses args, resolves the revset, and decides which path to take. If the user used `..` notation (e.g. `vc-x1 show rev..`), it delegates to `show_both`. Otherwise it loops over the resolved commit IDs itself, calling `show_one_commit` for each.

- **`show_one_commit`** (line 209) — the **leaf renderer**. It prints all the detail for a single commit: IDs, author, committer, parents, children, branches, follows/precedes tags, description, and changed files. Both `show` and `show_both` call this.

- **`show_both`** (line 133) — handles the **`..` (both-directions) case**. When the user writes e.g. `vc-x1 show @.. 3`, they want to see commits both above and below the anchor. `show_both` resolves descendants (`rev::`) and ancestors (`::rev`) separately, takes `both_count` from each direction, then renders them in order: descendants first, then the anchor (marked primary), then ancestors — each via `show_one_commit`.

In short: `show` is the dispatcher, `show_one_commit` is the single-commit renderer, and `show_both` is the special-case orchestrator for bidirectional `..` queries. The split keeps the bidirectional logic (which needs separate ancestor/descendant revset resolution) out of the normal single-direction loop.

Now I have enough information. Let me create a comprehensive analysis document:

## Analysis: `..` (Both) Notation Handling Across Subcommands

### Pattern Overview

All four subcommands follow a **consistent structural pattern** for handling the `..` notation, with the key decision point being whether `both_count` is set:

```
resolve_dot_args → check both_count → if Some, call collect_both → else, iterate normally
```

---

### 1. **Common Parsing & Resolution** (shared by all)

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/common.rs`

**Parsing (`parse_dot_rev`):**
- Strips `..` prefixes/suffixes to extract direction (`Both | Ancestors | Descendants | None`)
- Returns `DotSpec { rev, direction, count: 0 }`

**Resolution (`resolve_dot_args`):**
- Takes positional args (`pos_rev`, `pos_count`) + named flags (`-r`, `-l`)
- For `DotDirection::Both` → returns `ResolvedArgs { revset: "::x|x::", both_count: Some(count), ... }`
- For other directions → returns `revset: "::x"` or `"x::"` with explicit `limit`
- Sets `both_count: None` for non-Both cases

---

### 2. **Detection Pattern** (in all four subcommands)

Each subcommand checks the same condition **early**:

```rust
if let Some(both_count) = resolved.both_count {
    // BOTH path
    return early;
}
// Normal path (ancestors/descendants/single)
```

**All four files show this at line ~50:**
- `chid.rs:50`
- `desc.rs:50`
- `list.rs:50`
- `show.rs:92` (with minor structural difference)

---

### 3. **Collection: The Duplicated `collect_both` Pattern**

**Three subcommands use `common::collect_both()`:**
- `chid.rs` (lines 51–66)
- `desc.rs` (lines 51–66)
- `list.rs` (lines 51–66)

**`show.rs` duplicates logic inline** in `show_both()` function (lines 133–207):
- Manually resolves `::x` and `x::` revsets
- Filters and slices descendants and ancestors separately
- Iterates in the same order: descendants, anchor, ancestors

### Key Logic (from `common::collect_both`, lines 281–345):

```rust
let ancestor_ids = resolve_revset(..., "::x")?;
let descendant_ids = resolve_revset(..., "x::")?;

// Descendants: filter root and anchor, keep newest (from jj order) to oldest
// If both_count > 0: take last N (most recent N)
let mut desc_lines = [...];
if both_count > 0 {
    let start = desc_lines.len().saturating_sub(both_count);
    desc_lines = desc_lines[start..].to_vec();  // Last both_count
} else {
    desc_lines.clear();  // No count = show nothing
}

// Ancestors: filter root and anchor, iterate up to both_count
let mut anc_lines = [...];
if both_count > 0 {
    let mut count = 0;
    for commit_id in &ancestor_ids {
        // ...
        count += 1;
        if count >= both_count { break; }
    }
}

let anchor_index = desc_lines.len();  // Position of anchor in final array
let mut result = desc_lines;
result.push(formatter(&anchor_commit));
result.extend(anc_lines);
Ok((result, anchor_index))
```

---

### 4. **What Differs** (per-subcommand)

#### **chid.rs** (lines 51–66)
- Calls `collect_both(..., common::format_chid)`
- Output: bold-highlighted **anchor only** (index lookup)
- Formatting: changeID only

#### **desc.rs** (lines 51–66)
- Calls `collect_both(..., common::format_commit_full)`
- Output: bold first line of **anchor only** (via `bold_first_line`)
- Formatting: changeID + commitID + full description (multi-line)
- Post-processing: indents lines by 4 spaces

#### **list.rs** (lines 51–66)
- Calls `collect_both(..., common::format_commit_short)`
- Output: bold **anchor only**
- Formatting: changeID + commitID + first line

#### **show.rs** (lines 92–102 main; 133–207 `show_both`)
- **Duplicates collection logic** (does not call `collect_both`)
- Output: **full commit details for all**—descendants, anchor (bold IDs), ancestors
- Separators: `────────────────────────────────────────` between commits
- Formatting: headers (Ids, Author, Committer, Parents, Children, Branches, Follows, Precedes, Description, Changed files)
- Inline logic matches `collect_both` structure but no line array → direct printing

---

### 5. **Structural Duplication & Differences**

| Aspect | chid, desc, list | show |
|--------|------------------|------|
| Detection | `if both_count.is_some()` (line ~50) | Same (line 92) |
| Collection | `collect_both()` helper | Inline in `show_both()` |
| Revset Resolution | Implicit in helper | Explicit: `::x`, `x::` |
| Filtering | In helper | In function body (lines 149–178) |
| Output Scope | Only anchor (bold) | All commits (desc/anc/anchor) |
| Per-commit Rendering | Lightweight (1 line) | Heavy (10+ lines per commit) |
| Early Return | Yes | Yes |

---

### 6. **Key Insight: The Asymmetry**

The **data structure** is identical across all subcommands:
- Resolve ancestors from `::x` (oldest-to-newest)
- Resolve descendants from `x::` (oldest-to-newest)
- Slice descendants to last `both_count` (most recent)
- Iterate ancestors to first `both_count`
- Construct: `[desc...] + [anchor] + [anc...]`

But the **output scope** differs:
- **chid, desc, list**: Only highlight/format the anchor (the primary rev)
- **show**: Render complete details for all displayed commits

`show.rs` has duplication because it needs to:
1. Skip the `collect_both` string-building overhead (it renders directly)
2. Insert separators between commits (not handled by `collect_both`)
3. Call different per-commit renderers per commit (not just one formatter)

---

- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/common.rs` — `resolve_dot_args`, `collect_both`, parsing

- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/desc.rs` — lines 50–66 (collect_both usage)
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/list.rs` — lines 50–66 (collect_both usage)
- `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/show.rs` — lines 92–102 (detection) + 133–207 (duplication)

> AGENT

Great analysis. Here's what it reveals:

**The duplication:** `show_both` (lines 133–207) manually re-implements the same ancestor/descendant collection logic that `common::collect_both` already provides for chid, desc, and list. The pattern is identical — resolve `::rev` and `rev::`, filter root/anchor, slice to `both_count`, order as descendants → anchor → ancestors.

**Why `show` diverged:** `collect_both` takes a formatter closure that returns strings, then returns a `Vec<String>`. `show` needs to call `show_one_commit` (which prints headers, branches, tags, diffs directly) — it can't reduce a commit to a single string. So it duplicated the collection logic to work with `Commit` objects directly.

**The refactoring opportunity:** Instead of `show_both`, you could:

1. **Extract a generic `collect_both_commits`** in `common.rs` that returns the ordered `Vec<CommitId>` (descendants + anchor + ancestors) with the anchor index — no formatting, just the collection/ordering logic. The existing string-based `collect_both` could be built on top of it.

2. **Replace `show_both` with `show_ancestors` / `show_descendants`** as you suggested — but honestly the ancestor vs descendant logic isn't that different (just which revset and which slicing). The real value is pulling the shared collection into common and having `show` call it like the others do.

The cleanest approach would be something like:

```rust
// common.rs
fn collect_both_ids(workspace, repo, rev, both_count)
    -> Result<(Vec<CommitId>, usize)>  // (ordered IDs, anchor_index)

// Then collect_both() becomes a thin wrapper that maps IDs through a formatter
// And show.rs just calls collect_both_ids + iterates with show_one_commit
```

This eliminates ~75 lines of duplication in `show_both` and makes the `..` handling truly shared. The per-commit rendering stays in each subcommand where it belongs.

    21→/// Direction(s) indicated by `..` notation.
    22→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    23→pub enum DotDirection {
    24→    /// No dots: just the revision itself.
    25→    None,
    26→    /// `x..` — ancestors (x at top, older below).
    27→    Ancestors,
    28→    /// `..x` — descendants (newer above, x at bottom).
    29→    Descendants,
    30→    /// `..x..` — both directions, x in the middle.
    31→    Both,
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Result of parsing positional `..` notation.
    35→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    36→pub struct DotSpec {
    37→    /// The bare revision (without `..`).
    38→    pub rev: String,
    39→    /// Which direction(s) the dots indicate.
    40→    pub direction: DotDirection,
    41→    /// Count per dotted side (x is not counted).
    42→    pub count: usize,
    43→}
    44→
    45→/// Parse a positional REV string for `..` notation.
    46→///
    47→/// Returns the bare revision, direction, and default count (0).
    48→/// Count is set separately from the second positional arg.
    49→pub fn parse_dot_rev(rev: &str) -> DotSpec {
    50→    if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..").and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix("..")) {
    51→        DotSpec {
    52→            rev: inner.to_string(),
    53→            direction: DotDirection::Both,
    54→            count: 0,
    55→        }
    56→    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_prefix("..") {
    57→        DotSpec {
    58→            rev: inner.to_string(),
    59→            direction: DotDirection::Descendants,
    60→            count: 0,
    61→        }
    62→    } else if let Some(inner) = rev.strip_suffix("..") {
    63→        DotSpec {
    64→            rev: inner.to_string(),
    65→            direction: DotDirection::Ancestors,
    66→            count: 0,
    67→        }
    68→    } else {
    69→        DotSpec {
    70→            rev: rev.to_string(),
    71→            direction: DotDirection::None,
    72→            count: 0,
    73→        }
    74→    }
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Resolve effective revision, limit, and whether results need reversing
    78→/// from positional args combined with named flags.
    79→///
    80→/// Named flags (`-r`, `-l`) take precedence over positional args.
    81→pub struct ResolvedArgs {
    82→    /// The jj revset string to evaluate.
    83→    pub revset: String,
    84→    /// Maximum number of commits to return (None = unlimited).
    85→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    86→    /// For `..x..`, the count per side.
    87→    pub both_count: Option<usize>,
    88→    /// The bare primary revision (for highlighting).
    89→    pub primary_rev: String,
    90→}
    91→
    92→/// Resolve positional `..` args with named flag overrides.
    93→///
    94→/// - `pos_rev`: first positional arg (may contain `..`)
    95→/// - `pos_count`: second positional arg
    96→/// - `flag_rev`: `-r` flag value (if different from default)
    97→/// - `flag_limit`: `-l` flag value
    98→/// - `default_rev`: the default revision (e.g. "@")
    99→pub fn resolve_dot_args(
   100→    pos_rev: Option<&str>,
   101→    pos_count: Option<usize>,
   102→    flag_rev: &str,
   103→    flag_limit: Option<usize>,
   104→    default_rev: &str,
   105→) -> ResolvedArgs {
   106→    // If named flags were explicitly set, they take full precedence
   107→    let flag_rev_set = flag_rev != default_rev;
   108→    let flag_limit_set = flag_limit.is_some();
   109→
   110→    if flag_rev_set || (flag_limit_set && pos_rev.is_none()) {
   111→        return ResolvedArgs {
   112→            revset: flag_rev.to_string(),
   113→            limit: flag_limit,
   114→            both_count: None,
   115→            primary_rev: flag_rev.to_string(),
   116→        };
   117→    }
   118→
   119→    // Use positional args
   120→    let rev_str = pos_rev.unwrap_or(default_rev);
   121→    let mut spec = parse_dot_rev(rev_str);
   122→
   123→    // If there's a positional count, set it
   124→    if let Some(c) = pos_count {
   125→        spec.count = c;
   126→    }
   127→
   128→    // Named limit overrides positional count
   129→    if flag_limit_set {
   130→        spec.count = flag_limit.unwrap();
   131→    }
   132→
   133→    let primary = spec.rev.clone();
   134→
   135→    match spec.direction {
   136→        DotDirection::None => {
   137→            if spec.count > 0 {
   138→                // bare `x 5` → `x.. 5` (ancestors)
   139→                ResolvedArgs {
   140→                    revset: format!("::{}", spec.rev),
   141→                    limit: Some(spec.count + 1), // +1 for x itself
   142→                    both_count: None,
   143→                    primary_rev: primary,
   144→                }
   145→            } else {
   146→                ResolvedArgs {
   147→                    revset: spec.rev,
   148→                    limit: Some(1),
   149→                    both_count: None,
   150→                    primary_rev: primary,
   151→                }
   152→            }
   153→        }
   154→        DotDirection::Ancestors => {
   155→            if spec.count > 0 {
   156→                ResolvedArgs {
   157→                    revset: format!("::{}", spec.rev),
   158→                    limit: Some(spec.count + 1),
   159→                    both_count: None,
   160→                    primary_rev: primary,
   161→                }
   162→            } else {
   163→                ResolvedArgs {
   164→                    revset: format!("::{}", spec.rev),
   165→                    limit: None,
   166→                    both_count: None,
   167→                    primary_rev: primary,
   168→                }
   169→            }
   170→        }
   171→        DotDirection::Descendants => {
   172→            if spec.count > 0 {
   173→                ResolvedArgs {
   174→                    revset: format!("{}::", spec.rev),
   175→                    limit: Some(spec.count + 1),
   176→                    both_count: None,
   177→                    primary_rev: primary,
   178→                }
   179→            } else {
   180→                ResolvedArgs {
   181→                    revset: format!("{}::", spec.rev),
   182→                    limit: None,
   183→                    both_count: None,
   184→                    primary_rev: primary,
   185→                }
   186→            }
   187→        }
   188→        DotDirection::Both => ResolvedArgs {
   189→            revset: format!("::{}|{}::", spec.rev, spec.rev),
   190→            limit: None,
   191→            both_count: Some(spec.count),
   192→            primary_rev: primary,
   193→        },
   194→    }
   195→}
   196→
   197→/// Wrap text in ANSI bold escape codes.
   198→pub fn bold(s: &str) -> String {
   199→    format!("\x1b[1m{s}\x1b[0m")
   200→}
   201→
   202→/// Bold only the first line of a multi-line string.
   203→pub fn bold_first_line(s: &str) -> String {
   204→    if let Some(pos) = s.find('\n') {
   205→        format!("\x1b[1m{}\x1b[0m{}", &s[..pos], &s[pos..])
   206→    } else {
   207→        bold(s)
   208→    }
   209→}
   210→
   211→/// Indent all lines after the first by `n` spaces.
   212→///
   213→/// Returns the string unchanged if `n == 0` or there is only one line.
   214→pub fn indent_body(s: &str, n: usize) -> String {
   215→    if n == 0 {
   216→        return s.to_string();
   217→    }
   218→    let pad = " ".repeat(n);
   219→    let mut lines = s.lines();
   220→    let mut result = match lines.next() {
   221→        Some(first) => first.to_string(),
   222→        None => return String::new(),
   223→    };
   224→    for line in lines {
   225→        result.push('\n');
   226→        if !line.is_empty() {
   227→            result.push_str(&pad);
   228→        }
   229→        result.push_str(line);
   230→    }
   231→    result
   232→}
   233→
   234→/// Format: just the short changeID.
   235→pub fn format_chid(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   236→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   237→    change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)].to_string()
   238→}
   239→
   240→/// Format: changeID commitID first-line (single line).
   241→pub fn format_commit_short(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   242→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   243→    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   244→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   245→    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
   246→    let first_line = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or("");
   247→    if first_line.is_empty() {
   248→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
   249→    } else {
   250→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}")
   251→    }
   252→}
   253→
   254→/// Format: changeID commitID first-line, then remaining description lines.
   255→pub fn format_commit_full(commit: &Commit) -> String {
   256→    let change_hex = encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   257→    let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   258→    let commit_hex = commit.id().hex();
   259→    let commit_short = &commit_hex[..commit_hex.len().min(12)];
   260→    let desc = commit.description();
   261→    if desc.is_empty() {
   262→        format!("{change_short} {commit_short} (no description set)")
   263→    } else {
   264→        let mut lines = desc.lines();
   265→        let first_line = lines.next().unwrap_or("");
   266→        let mut result = format!("{change_short} {commit_short} {first_line}");
   267→        for line in lines {
   268→            result.push('\n');
   269→            result.push_str(line);
   270→        }
   271→        result
   272→    }
   273→}
   274→
   275→/// Collect formatted lines for `..x..` (both directions).
   276→///
   277→/// `formatter` converts a `Commit` into a display string.
   278→/// Returns `(lines, anchor_index)` — the lines in display order
   279→/// (descendants newest first, anchor, ancestors newest first)
   280→/// and the index of the anchor commit within the lines.
   281→pub fn collect_both<F>(
   282→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   283→    repo_path: &Path,
   284→    pos_rev: Option<&str>,
   285→    both_count: usize,
   286→    formatter: F,
   287→) -> Result<(Vec<String>, usize), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>
   288→where
   289→    F: Fn(&Commit) -> String,
   290→{
   291→    let bare_rev = pos_rev.unwrap_or("@");
   292→    let spec = parse_dot_rev(bare_rev);
   293→    let (workspace, _) = load_repo(repo_path)?;
   294→    let anchor_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &spec.rev)?;
   295→    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {
   296→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", spec.rev).into());
   297→    }
   298→    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];
   299→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
   300→
   301→    let ancestor_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!("::{}", spec.rev))?;
   302→    let descendant_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!("{}::", spec.rev))?;
   303→
   304→    // Descendants (newest first from jj), excluding anchor
   305→    let mut desc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   306→    for commit_id in &descendant_ids {
   307→        if *commit_id == root_commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {
   308→            continue;
   309→        }
   310→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   311→        desc_lines.push(formatter(&commit));
   312→    }
   313→    if both_count > 0 {
   314→        let start = desc_lines.len().saturating_sub(both_count);
   315→        desc_lines = desc_lines[start..].to_vec();
   316→    } else {
   317→        desc_lines.clear();
   318→    }
   319→
   320→    // Anchor
   321→    let anchor_commit = repo.store().get_commit(anchor_id)?;
   322→
   323→    // Ancestors (newest first from jj), excluding anchor
   324→    let mut anc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   325→    if both_count > 0 {
   326→        let mut count = 0;
   327→        for commit_id in &ancestor_ids {
   328→            if root_commit_id == *commit_id || *commit_id == *anchor_id {
   329→                continue;
   330→            }
   331→            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   332→            anc_lines.push(formatter(&commit));
   333→            count += 1;
   334→            if count >= both_count {
   335→                break;
   336→            }
   337→        }
   338→    }
   339→
   340→    let anchor_index = desc_lines.len();
   341→    let mut result = desc_lines;
   342→    result.push(formatter(&anchor_commit));
   343→    result.extend(anc_lines);
   344→    Ok((result, anchor_index))
   345→}
   346→
   347→pub fn load_repo(
   348→    path: &Path,
   349→) -> Result<(Workspace, Arc<ReadonlyRepo>), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   350→    let config = StackedConfig::with_defaults();
   351→    let settings = UserSettings::from_config(config)?;
   352→    let store_factories = StoreFactories::default();
   353→    let working_copy_factories = default_working_copy_factories();
   354→
   355→    let workspace = Workspace::load(&settings, path, &store_factories, &working_copy_factories)?;
   356→    let repo = workspace.repo_loader().load_at_head().block_on()?;
   357→
   358→    Ok((workspace, repo))
   359→}
   360→
   361→/// Parse and evaluate a revset string, returning commit IDs.
   362→pub fn resolve_revset(
   363→    workspace: &Workspace,
   364→    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   365→    revset_str: &str,
   366→) -> Result<Vec<CommitId>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   367→    let aliases_map = RevsetAliasesMap::new();
   368→    let fileset_aliases_map = FilesetAliasesMap::new();
   369→    let extensions = RevsetExtensions::default();
   370→    let workspace_root = workspace.workspace_root().to_path_buf();
   371→    let path_converter = RepoPathUiConverter::Fs {
   372→        cwd: workspace_root.clone(),
   373→        base: workspace_root,
   374→    };
   375→    let workspace_name = workspace.workspace_name();
   376→    let workspace_ctx = RevsetWorkspaceContext {
   377→        path_converter: &path_converter,
   378→        workspace_name,
   379→    };
   380→    let context = RevsetParseContext {
   381→        aliases_map: &aliases_map,
   382→        local_variables: HashMap::new(),
   383→        user_email: "",
   384→        date_pattern_context: chrono::Utc::now().fixed_offset().into(),
   385→        default_ignored_remote: None,
   386→        fileset_aliases_map: &fileset_aliases_map,
   387→        use_glob_by_default: false,
   388→        extensions: &extensions,
   389→        workspace: Some(workspace_ctx),
   390→    };
   391→
   392→    let mut diagnostics = RevsetDiagnostics::new();
   393→    let expression = jj_lib::revset::parse(&mut diagnostics, revset_str, &context)?;
   394→
   395→    let no_extensions: &[Box<dyn jj_lib::revset::SymbolResolverExtension>] = &[];
   396→    let symbol_resolver = SymbolResolver::new(repo.as_ref(), no_extensions);
   397→    let resolved = expression.resolve_user_expression(repo.as_ref(), &symbol_resolver)?;
   398→    let revset = resolved.evaluate(repo.as_ref())?;
   399→
   400→    let mut commit_ids = Vec::new();
   401→    for result in revset.iter() {
   402→        commit_ids.push(result?);
   403→    }
   404→    Ok(commit_ids)
   405→}
   406→

     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→
     3→use clap::Args;
     4→use jj_lib::repo::Repo;
     5→
     6→use crate::common;
     7→
     8→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
     9→pub struct ChidArgs {
    10→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    11→    #[arg()]
    12→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    13→
    14→    /// Count (items per dotted side)
    15→    #[arg()]
    16→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    17→
    18→    /// Revision to look up (default: @)
    19→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    20→    pub revision: String,
    21→
    22→    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)
    23→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    24→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    25→
    26→    /// Maximum number of changeIDs to show
    27→    #[arg(short, long)]
    28→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    29→}
    30→
    31→pub fn chid(args: &ChidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    32→    let resolved = common::resolve_dot_args(
    33→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    34→        args.pos_count,
    35→        &args.revision,
    36→        args.limit,
    37→        "@",
    38→    );
    39→
    40→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    41→
    42→    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.revset)?;
    43→
    44→    if commit_ids.is_empty() {
    45→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", resolved.revset).into());
    46→    }
    47→
    48→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
    49→
    50→    if let Some(both_count) = resolved.both_count {
    51→        let (lines, anchor) = common::collect_both(
    52→            &repo,
    53→            &args.repo,
    54→            args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    55→            both_count,
    56→            common::format_chid,
    57→        )?;
    58→        for (i, line) in lines.iter().enumerate() {
    59→            if i == anchor {
    60→                println!("{}", common::bold(line));
    61→            } else {
    62→                println!("{line}");
    63→            }
    64→        }
    65→        return Ok(());
    66→    }
    67→
    68→    // Resolve the primary commit ID for highlighting
    69→    let primary_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.primary_rev)?;
    70→    let primary_id = primary_ids.first();
    71→
    72→    let mut results: Vec<(String, bool)> = Vec::new();
    73→    let mut count = 0;
    74→    for commit_id in &commit_ids {
    75→        if *commit_id == root_commit_id {
    76→            continue;
    77→        }
    78→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
    79→        let is_primary = primary_id == Some(commit_id);
    80→        results.push((common::format_chid(&commit), is_primary));
    81→
    82→        count += 1;
    83→        if let Some(limit) = resolved.limit
    84→            && count >= limit
    85→        {
    86→            break;
    87→        }
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    for (line, is_primary) in &results {
    91→        if *is_primary {
    92→            println!("{}", common::bold(line));
    93→        } else {
    94→            println!("{line}");
    95→        }
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    Ok(())
    99→}

     1→# Chores-02
     2→
     3→## Graphical tools for jj repos
     4→
     5→### GG (gg)
     6→
     7→Cross-platform GUI for jj (Tauri-based). Interactive graph view.
     8→
     9→- Install: https://github.com/gulbanana/gg
    10→- `gg gui` does NOT work; use `gg web` instead (opens in browser)
    11→
    12→### jj-fzf
    13→
    14→fzf-based TUI centered on `jj log` graph. Previews diffs, evolog,
    15→op log. Many keybindings for common jj operations.
    16→
    17→- Install: https://github.com/tim-janik/jj-fzf
    18→- Requires `fzf` installed separately (`sudo pacman -S fzf` on Arch)
    19→
    20→### Others (not tested)
    21→
    22→- **jjui** — TUI for jj (https://github.com/idursun/jjui)
    23→- **jjgui** — commercial desktop app (https://jj-gui.com/)
    24→- **Jujutsu Kaizen** — VS Code plugin
    25→- **VisualJJ** — VS Code plugin (native jj, no git colocation)
    26→- **Selvejj** — JetBrains plugin
    27→
    28→### Built-in
    29→
    30→- `jj log` — shows graph by default (no `--graph` flag needed)
    31→- `gitk --all` — works on the colocated `.git` but sees raw git refs,
    32→  not jj's view of history
    33→
    34→## jj commit organization and traversal mechanisms
    35→
    36→### Motivation
    37→
    38→Understanding how jj commits are organized and what jj-lib APIs exist
    39→for traversing them — a prerequisite for building richer views of
    40→codebase evolution over time. The todo item "determine the organization
    41→of commits in jj and how we can iterate over them" prompted this
    42→investigation.
    43→
    44→### Three axes of traversal
    45→
    46→jj provides three distinct ways to navigate commit history:
    47→
    48→1. **Commit DAG** — parent/child relationships between commits
    49→2. **Refs/bookmarks** — named pointers (bookmarks, tags, git refs) into the DAG
    50→3. **Evolution log (evolog)** — rewrite history of a single ChangeId across operations
    51→
    52→Each answers a different question:
    53→- DAG: "what is the commit history?"
    54→- Refs: "where are the named branches pointing?"
    55→- Evolog: "how was this particular change built up?"
    56→
    57→### Commit DAG traversal via revsets
    58→
    59→jj-lib's revset engine supports both string-based and programmatic queries.
    60→
    61→**String-based** (what vc-x1 uses today via `common::resolve_revset`):
    62→```rust
    63→resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, "::@")?  // ancestors of @
    64→resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, "all()")? // all visible commits
    65→```
    66→
    67→**Programmatic** (builder API, no parsing needed):
    68→```rust
    69→use jj_lib::revset::RevsetExpression;
    70→
    71→RevsetExpression::all()                    // all visible + referenced
    72→RevsetExpression::visible_heads()          // tips of visible branches
    73→RevsetExpression::symbol("@".into())       // working copy
    74→RevsetExpression::commits(vec![id])        // explicit set
    75→RevsetExpression::bookmarks(pattern)       // bookmark refs
    76→RevsetExpression::git_refs()               // all git refs
    77→
    78→// Chainable traversal
    79→expr.ancestors()           // ::x
    80→expr.descendants()         // x::
    81→expr.parents()             // direct parents
    82→expr.children()            // direct children
    83→expr.heads()               // maximal elements
    84→expr.roots()               // minimal elements
    85→expr.fork_point()          // merge base
    86→
    87→// Ranges and set operations
    88→expr1.range(&expr2)        // x..y
    89→expr1.dag_range_to(&expr2) // x::y
    90→expr1.union(&expr2)
    91→expr1.intersection(&expr2)
    92→expr1.minus(&expr2)
    93→```
    94→
    95→### Iteration modes
    96→
    97→Evaluated revsets support three iteration modes:
    98→
    99→```rust
   100→let revset = expr.evaluate(repo)?;
   101→
   102→revset.iter()               // yields CommitId in topo order (newest first)
   103→revset.commit_change_ids()  // yields (CommitId, ChangeId) pairs
   104→revset.iter_graph()         // yields GraphNode<CommitId> with edge info
   105→```
   106→
   107→`iter_graph()` provides `GraphEdge` data with edge types (`Direct`,
   108→`Indirect`, `Missing`) — needed for rendering graph views like
   109→`jj log --graph`.
   110→
   111→### Ref/bookmark access via View
   112→
   113→```rust
   114→let view = repo.view();
   115→view.heads()              // HashSet<CommitId> — visible branch tips
   116→view.bookmarks()          // all bookmarks with local+remote state
   117→view.local_bookmarks()    // local bookmarks only
   118→view.git_refs()           // BTreeMap of git refs
   119→view.git_head()           // git HEAD
   120→```
   121→
   122→### Index (direct DAG queries)
   123→
   124→```rust
   125→let index = repo.index();
   126→index.is_ancestor(&id1, &id2)?           // ancestry check
   127→index.common_ancestors(&[id1], &[id2])?  // merge base
   128→index.heads(&mut candidates)?            // compute heads
   129→index.all_heads_for_gc()?                // every head including hidden
   130→```
   131→
   132→### Low-level DAG walks (dag_walk module)
   133→
   134→Generic traversal functions on arbitrary DAGs:
   135→
   136→```rust
   137→jj_lib::dag_walk::dfs(start, id_fn, neighbors_fn)
   138→jj_lib::dag_walk::topo_order_forward(...)
   139→jj_lib::dag_walk::topo_order_reverse(...)
   140→jj_lib::dag_walk::heads(...)
   141→jj_lib::dag_walk::closest_common_node(...)
   142→```
   143→
   144→### Evolution log (evolog)
   145→
   146→The evolog shows every version a ChangeId went through — snapshots,
   147→squashes, rebases, describes. It's tracked at the **operation level**,
   148→not in the commit DAG.
   149→
   150→Example output from `jj evolog -r @-`:
   151→```
   152→◆    rpyrwwnw wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:53:12 main f7f567f6
   153→├─╮  Finalize: replace --foreground with --detach, ...
   154→│ │  -- operation 655ff50a3386 squash commits into ...
   155→│ ○  tluyvppo/0 wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:53:12 89b1c104 (hidden)
   156→│ │  (no description set)
   157→│ │  -- operation e71fa874e37a snapshot working copy
   158→│ ○  tluyvppo/1 wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:49:54 317cefcf (hidden)
   159→│    (empty) (no description set)
   160→│    -- operation e1fe385617e5 commit ...
   161→○  rpyrwwnw/1 wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:49:54 50c30496 (hidden)
   162→│  Finalize: replace --foreground with --detach, ...
   163→...
   164→```
   165→
   166→**Core API** (`jj_lib::evolution`):
   167→
   168→```rust
   169→use jj_lib::evolution::{walk_predecessors, CommitEvolutionEntry};
   170→
   171→// Walk the rewrite history of a commit
   172→let iter = walk_predecessors(repo, &[commit_id]);
   173→for entry in iter {
   174→    let entry: CommitEvolutionEntry = entry?;
   175→    // entry.commit        — the commit at this point in history
   176→    // entry.operation      — the operation that created this version
   177→    // entry.predecessor_ids() — IDs of the previous version(s)
   178→    // entry.predecessors()    — iterator over predecessor Commit objects
   179→}
   180→
   181→// Batch resolve predecessors across operation ranges
   182→let map: BTreeMap<CommitId, Vec<CommitId>> =
   183→    accumulate_predecessors(new_ops, old_ops)?;
   184→```
   185→
   186→### Evolog data is local-only
   187→
   188→**Critical finding**: operation/evolution data does NOT survive a git
   189→push + clone cycle. This is by design.
   190→
   191→From `op_store.rs`:
   192→> "Operations and views are not meant to be exchanged between repos or
   193→> users; they represent local state and history."
   194→
   195→| Data                  | Pushed? | Recoverable on clone? |
   196→|-----------------------|---------|----------------------|
   197→| Git commits + trees   | Yes     | Yes                  |
   198→| Change IDs            | No      | Yes (from git header)|
   199→| Commit predecessors   | No      | No                   |
   200→| Operation history     | No      | No                   |
   201→| Evolution log         | No      | No                   |
   202→
   203→Predecessor tracking is stored per-operation in
   204→`op_store::Operation::commit_predecessors` (since jj 0.30). The old
   205→`backend::Commit::predecessors` field is deprecated and slated for
   206→removal around jj 0.42.
   207→
   208→### Implications for codebase evolution story
   209→
   210→The evolog provides a rich local narrative of how changes were built
   211→up — intermediate snapshots, false starts, squashes — that commit
   212→messages alone don't capture. However, this data is ephemeral across
   213→git transport.
   214→
   215→For the dual-repo workflow (app + `.claude`), the `.claude` repo's
   216→evolog captures how session commits were constructed. If `.claude`
   217→eventually becomes a git submodule or is backed by a database, the
   218→evolog data could be extracted and persisted via `walk_predecessors()`
   219→before it's lost to a clone. This would preserve the full provenance
   220→chain: not just what changed, but how the change was assembled.
   221→
   222→## Interactive DAG navigation: the missing feature (2026-03-18)
   223→
   224→### The problem
   225→
   226→When working with jj-git repos, especially complex ones, you need to
   227→navigate the commit DAG interactively — click a parent or child and
   228→jump to it instantly. This is table-stakes in gitk but absent from
   229→jj-native tooling.
   230→
   231→### gitk: still the best for DAG traversal
   232→
   233→gitk's killer feature: the parent/child list lets you **click-to-jump**
   234→to any commit, even if it's far off-screen. True graph traversal, not
   235→scrolling. Works in colocated jj repos via `jj git export`.
   236→
   237→**Downsides:** Shows Git's view only — no change IDs, no revsets, no
   238→jj bookmarks. Detached HEAD display is confusing. Cannot perform jj
   239→operations. Does not work with non-colocated jj repos.
   240→
   241→### gg-cli: close but not there
   242→
   243→Renders a true graphical DAG. Can show parents in the detail pane and
   244→click to select a parent — but **only if that parent is already visible
   245→in the left panel**. If the parent is off-screen, there is no jump-to
   246→behavior; you must manually scroll using the slider. This makes it
   247→**useless on large/complex repos** for DAG traversal.
   248→
   249→Drag-and-drop rebase is nice, but the lack of click-to-jump is a
   250→dealbreaker for navigation.
   251→
   252→### Other tools: list navigators, not graph navigators
   253→
   254→- **jj-fzf** — displays `jj log` graph in fzf with live revset editing.
   255→  Navigation is list-based (up/down), not graph-based (follow edges).
   256→- **jjui** — revision tree view, growing fast, but still fundamentally
   257→  a list navigator with a graph drawn alongside.
   258→- **lazyjj** — operations-focused TUI, not DAG-focused.
   259→- **jjdag** — pre-alpha, explicitly DAG-focused (Magit-style keys,
   260→  foldable tree). Worth watching.
   261→- **jj log** — revset language is powerful for querying (`children(x)`,
   262→  `ancestors(main..@)`) but output is static, not interactive.
   263→
   264→### Comparison
   265→
   266→| Tool     | DAG display | Click-to-jump parent/child | Native jj |
   267→|----------|-------------|---------------------------|-----------|
   268→| gitk     | graphical   | **yes** (both directions)  | no        |
   269→| gg-cli   | graphical   | parent only, no jump if off-screen | yes |
   270→| jj-fzf   | ASCII       | no                         | yes       |
   271→| jjui     | ASCII       | no                         | yes       |
   272→| jj log   | ASCII       | n/a (not interactive)      | yes       |
   273→
   274→### Conclusion
   275→
   276→**gitk remains the best tool for interactive DAG traversal** despite
   277→its jj blindspots. The jj ecosystem is missing a tool that combines
   278→jj-native concepts (change IDs, revsets, bookmarks) with gitk-style
   279→click-to-jump on parent/child links. This would be a strong feature
   280→request for gg-cli, which already has the infrastructure but lacks the
   281→jump-to behavior.
   282→
   283→### Cautionary tale: multi-tool dangers
   284→
   285→While testing jj-fzf and gg-cli simultaneously (with Zed editor also
   286→open), it's easy to accidentally move `@` to an unexpected commit via
   287→one tool without noticing. When Zed then sees files disappear from the
   288→working copy, closing a "changed" file and declining to save effectively
   289→deletes it. Recovery: `jj new main` to repoint `@`, or
   290→`jj file show <path> -r <rev> --at-op <op>` to extract from history.
   291→
   292→## jj-lib Commit API and tree storage model (2026-03-18)
   293→
   294→### What's on the Commit type
   295→
   296→Given a `Commit` (from `repo.store().get_commit(&commit_id)?`), the
   297→public API includes:
   298→
   299→| Method | Returns | Cost |
   300→|--------|---------|------|
   301→| `id()` | `&CommitId` | zero — stored in struct |
   302→| `change_id()` | `&ChangeId` | zero |
   303→| `description()` | `&str` | zero |
   304→| `author()` / `committer()` | `&Signature` | zero |
   305→| `parent_ids()` | `&[CommitId]` | zero — stored in commit data |
   306→| `parents()` | `Vec<Commit>` | async, loads parent commits |
   307→| `tree()` | `MergedTree` | near-zero — wraps tree IDs, no I/O |
   308→| `tree_ids()` | `&Merge<TreeId>` | zero |
   309→| `parent_tree(repo)` | `MergedTree` | async, merges parent trees |
   310→| `is_empty(repo)` | `bool` | compares tree to parent tree |
   311→| `has_conflict()` | `bool` | checks if tree IDs are conflicted |
   312→
   313→### What's NOT on Commit
   314→
   315→- **Children** — parent→child is a reverse lookup. Requires revsets:
   316→  ```rust
   317→  resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &format!("children({})", change_id))?
   318→  ```
   319→  Parents are stored in the commit; children require scanning the DAG.
   320→
   321→- **Diff/changes** — no method returns "what changed in this commit."
   322→  Must be computed by comparing two tree snapshots.
   323→
   324→### Getting diffs between a commit and its parent
   325→
   326→```rust
   327→use jj_lib::matchers::EverythingMatcher;
   328→
   329→let parent_tree = commit.parent_tree(repo).await?;
   330→let commit_tree = commit.tree();
   331→
   332→let mut diff_stream = parent_tree.diff_stream(&commit_tree, &EverythingMatcher);
   333→while let Some(entry) = diff_stream.next().await {
   334→    // entry.path  — RepoPathBuf (the file that changed)
   335→    // entry.values — Diff { before, after } of MergedTreeValue
   336→}
   337→```
   338→
   339→`parent_tree()` handles merge commits by merging multiple parent trees.
   340→
   341→### What's stored on disk
   342→
   343→jj uses git as its backend. On disk: git's content-addressable objects.
   344→
   345→- **Blobs** — file contents, deduplicated by SHA
   346→- **Trees** — directory listings (pointers to blobs and sub-trees)
   347→- **Commits** — pointer to root tree + parent IDs + metadata
   348→
   349→Each commit points to a **full tree snapshot**, but the tree is composed
   350→of shared objects. If a file didn't change, both commits' trees point to
   351→the same blob. Git also delta-compresses objects in packfiles.
   352→
   353→The "full snapshot" is **logical, not physical**. `commit.tree()` just
   354→wraps the root tree hash — no I/O happens until you traverse.
   355→
   356→### Cost model for tree operations
   357→
   358→| Operation | Cost | Why |
   359→|-----------|------|-----|
   360→| `commit.tree()` | near zero | wraps tree IDs, no I/O |
   361→| `tree.path_value(&path)` | O(path depth) | walks ~4-5 tree nodes |
   362→| `tree.diff_stream(&other)` | O(changed subtrees) | skips identical subtrees by hash |
   363→| `tree.entries()` | O(entire repo) | walks everything — avoid on large repos |
   364→
   365→The diff algorithm compares tree entries level by level. If a subtree
   366→hash matches, it's skipped entirely — never descended into. On a Linux
   367→monorepo commit that touches 5 files, it loads ~20-30 tree objects out
   368→of millions.
   369→
   370→The expensive part of a gitk-style patch view is reading **blob
   371→contents** for the line-by-line diff, but only for changed files.
   372→
   373→### jj-lib vs gitk efficiency
   374→
   375→jj-lib is a separate Rust implementation reading the same `.git/objects/`
   376→and packfiles — it does NOT shell out to git or link libgit2.
   377→
   378→The tree diff uses the **same hash-based short-circuiting** as git:
   379→`TreeDiffIterator` (in `merged_tree.rs`) checks `is_changed()` on
   380→`MergedTreeValue` (which compares tree hashes) and only recurses into
   381→directories that differ.
   382→
   383→jj-lib also has a feature gitk lacks: **concurrent tree loading**. When
   384→`store.concurrency() > 1`, it uses `TreeDiffStreamImpl` with async
   385→parallel I/O for loading tree objects, potentially faster than gitk on
   386→large repos.
   387→
   388→Source: `jj-lib-0.39.0/src/merged_tree.rs`, lines 260-690.
   389→
   390→### Key jj-lib source files (0.39.0)
   391→
   392→- `src/revset.rs` — revset expression types, builders, parsing
   393→- `src/evolution.rs` — evolog/predecessor traversal
   394→- `src/graph.rs` — graph structures, TopoGroupedGraphIterator
   395→- `src/dag_walk.rs` — generic DAG traversal (DFS, topo sort)
   396→- `src/repo.rs` — Repo trait, ReadonlyRepo
   397→- `src/view.rs` — View (heads, bookmarks, git refs)
   398→- `src/index.rs` — Index trait (ancestry, common ancestors)
   399→- `src/commit.rs` — Commit wrapper (parent_ids, change_id, description)
   400→- `src/backend.rs` — backend data types (CommitId, ChangeId, Signature)
   401→- `src/op_store.rs` — operation storage (commit_predecessors)
   402→- `src/operation.rs` — Operation type (predecessors_for_commit)
   403→
```

   // Anchor\n    let anchor_commit = repo.store().get_commit(anchor_id)?;\n\n    // Ancestors (newest first from jj), excluding anchor\n    let mut anc_lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    if newest first)\n/// and the index of the anchor commit within the list.\npub fn collect_ids(\n    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n    repo_path: &Path,\n    pos_rev: Option<&str>,\n    both_count: usize,\n) -> Result<(Vec<CommitId>, usize), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let bare_rev = pos_rev.unwrap_or(\"@\");\n    let spec = parse_dot_rev(bare_rev);\n    let (workspace, _) = load_repo(repo_path)?;\n    let anchor_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &spec.rev)?;\n    if anchor_ids.is_empty() {\n        return Err(format!(\"no commit found for revision '{}'\", spec.rev).into());\n    }\n    let anchor_id = &anchor_ids[0];\n    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n\n    let descendant_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!(\"{}::\", spec.rev))?;\n\n    // Descendants, excluding root and anchor\n    let mut desc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = descendant_ids\n        .into_iter()\n        .filter(|id| *id != root_commit_id && *id != *anchor_id)\n        .collect();\n    if both_count > 0 {\n        let start = desc_ids.len().saturating_sub(both_count);\n        desc_ids = desc_ids[start..].to_vec();\n    } else {\n        desc_ids.clear();\n    }\n\n    // Ancestors, excluding root and anchor, up to both_count\n    let mut anc_ids: Vec<CommitId> = Vec::new();\n    if both_count > 0 {\n        let ancestor_ids = resolve_revset(&workspace, repo, &format!(\"::{}\", spec.rev))?;\n        let mut count = 0;\n        for commit_id in ancestor_ids {\n            if commit_id == root_commit_id || commit_id == *anchor_id {\n                continue;\n            }\n            anc_ids.push(commit_id);\n            count += 1;\n            if count >= both_count {\n                break;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    let anchor_index = desc_ids.len();\n    let mut result = desc_ids;\n    result.push(anchor_id.clone());\n    result.extend(anc_ids);\n    Ok((result, anchor_index))\n}\n\n/// Collect formatted lines for `..x..` (both directions).\n///\n/// `formatter` converts a `Commit` into a display string.\n/// Returns `(lines, anchor_index)` — the lines in display order\n/// (descendants newest first, anchor, ancestors newest first)\n/// and the index of the anchor commit within the lines.\npub fn collect_commit_lines<F>(\n    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n    repo_path: &Path,\n    pos_rev: Option<&str>,\n    both_count: usize,\n    formatter: F,\n) -> Result<(Vec<String>, usize), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>\nwhere\n    F: Fn(&Commit) -> String,\n{\n    let (ids, anchor_index) = collect_ids(repo, repo_path, pos_rev, both_count)?;\n    let lines: Vec<String> = ids\n        .iter()\n        .map(|id| {\n            let commit = repo.store().get_commit(id)?;\n            Ok(formatter(&commit))\n        })\n        .collect::<Result<_, Box<dyn std::error::Error>>>()?;\n    Ok((lines, anchor_index))\n}"
}

> AGENT

You're right. The pattern is the same across all four cases — collect some descendants, the anchor, some ancestors — and the anchor_index tells you where the primary revision sits:

| Notation | Descendants | Anchor | Ancestors | anchor_index |
|----------|-------------|--------|-----------|-------------|
| `x`      | 0           | x      | 0         | 0           |
| `x.. 3`  | 0           | x      | 3         | 0           |
| `..x 3`  | 3           | x      | 0         | 3           |
| `..x.. 3`| 3           | x      | 3         | 3           |

So `collect_ids` could handle all cases if it takes descendant count and ancestor count separately instead of assuming Both. Then each subcommand's main function collapses to: call `collect_ids`, iterate, highlight at `anchor_index`. The `if let Some(both_count)` branch disappears entirely.

Want me to go that direction? The signature would be something like:

```rust
pub fn collect_ids(
    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
    repo_path: &Path,
    rev: &str,
    desc_count: Option<usize>,  // None = unlimited
    anc_count: Option<usize>,   // None = unlimited
) -> Result<(Vec<CommitId>, usize), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>
```

`anc_count`: `Some(n)` = n closest ancestors, `None` = all, `Some(0)` = none\npub fn collect_ids(\n    workspace: &Workspace,\n    repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n    rev: &str,\n    desc_count: Option<usize>,\n    anc_count: Option<usize>,\n) -> Result<(Vec<CommitId>, usize), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let anchor_ids = resolve_revset(workspace, repo, rev)?;\n    if     16→
    17→/// Parsed file limit: None (suppress), Some(n) (cap at n), or all.
    18→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
    19→pub enum FileLimit {
    20→    /// Don't show changed files section at all.
    21→    None,
    22→    /// Show up to N files (with first/last split if truncated).
    23→    Cap(usize),
    24→    /// Show all files.
    25→    All,
    26→}
    27→
    28→impl FileLimit {
    29→    fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Self, String> {
    30→        match s {
    31→            "0" => Ok(FileLimit::None),
    32→            "all" => Ok(FileLimit::All),
    33→            _ => s
    34→                .parse::<usize>()
    35→                .map(FileLimit::Cap)
    36→                .map_err(|_| format!("invalid file limit '{s}': expected number, 0, or 'all'")),
    37→        }
    38→    }
    39→}
    40→
    41→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
    42→pub struct ShowArgs {
    43→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    44→    #[arg()]
    45→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    46→
    47→    /// Count (commits per dotted side)
    48→    #[arg()]
    49→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    50→
    51→    /// Revision to show (default: @)
    52→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    53→    pub revision: String,
    54→
    55→    /// Max changed files: number, 0 (none), or 'all' (default: 50)
    56→    #[arg(short = 'f', long = "files", default_value = "50")]
    57→    pub files: String,
    58→
    59→    /// Maximum number of commits to show
    60→    #[arg(short, long)]
    61→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    62→
    63→    /// Path to jj repo (default: current directory)
    64→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    65→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    66→}
    67→
    68→pub fn show(args: &ShowArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    69→    let file_limit = FileLimit::parse(&args.files)?;
    70→
    71→    let resolved = common::resolve_dot_args(
    72→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
    73→        args.pos_count,
    74→        &args.revision,
    75→        args.limit,
    76→        "@",
    77→    );
    78→
    79→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
    80→    let commit_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.revset)?;
    81→
    82→    if commit_ids.is_empty() {
    83→        return Err(format!("no commit found for revision '{}'", resolved.revset).into());
    84→    }
    85→
    86→    let root_commit_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
    87→
    88→    // Resolve the primary commit ID for highlighting
    89→    let primary_ids = common::resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &resolved.primary_rev)?;
    90→    let primary_id = primary_ids.first();
    91→
    92→    if let Some(both_count) = resolved.both_count {
    93→        let count = if both_count > 0 {
    94→            Some(both_count)
    95→        } else {
    96→            Some(0)

  "new_string": "pub fn show(args: &ShowArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let file_limit = FileLimit::parse(&args.files)?;\n\n    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;\n\n    // Parse dot notation and map to collect_ids params\n    let flag_rev_set = args.revision != \"@\";\n    let bare_rev = args.pos_rev.as_deref().unwrap_or(\"@\");\n    let spec = common::parse_dot_rev(bare_rev);\n\n    let (rev, desc_count, anc_count) = if flag_rev_set {\n        // Flag override: just show the specified revision\n        (args.revision.as_str(), Some(0), args.limit.or(Some(0)))\n    } else {\n        match spec.direction {\n            common::DotDirection::None => {\n                let count = args.pos_count.or(args.limit);\n                (&*spec.rev, Some(0), count.or(Some(0)))\n            }\n            common::DotDirection::Ancestors => {\n                let count = args.pos_count.or(args.limit);\n                (&*spec.rev, Some(0), count)\n            }\n            common::DotDirection::Descendants => {\n                let count = args.pos_count.or(args.limit);\n                (&*spec.rev, count, Some(0))\n            }\n            common::DotDirection::Both => {\n                let count = args.pos_count.or(args.limit).or(Some(0));\n                (&*spec.rev, count, count)\n            }\n        }\n    };\n\n    let (ids, anchor_index) =\n        common::collect_ids(&workspace, &repo, rev, desc_count, anc_count)?;\n\n    let mut first = true;\n    for (i, commit_id) in ids.iter().enumerate() {\n        if !first {\n            println!(\"────────────────────────────────────────\");\n        }\n        first = false;\n\n        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;\n        show_one_commit(&commit, &workspace, &repo, file_limit, i == anchor_index)?;\n    }\n\n    Ok(())\n}"
}

How does this look to you? Ready to evaluate the approach before deciding on next steps?

    28→impl FileLimit {
